[01:55:34] The first one is talking about reinstating driver's license ceremonies. Studies show that the risk of vehicle accidents is exponentially higher for drivers age 16 to 18. Prior to the pandemic, Virginia law required driver's licenses for drivers younger than 18 to be sent to the Juvenile and Domestic Relations Court, better known as JDR. The JDR court would then conduct driver license ceremonies where a judge, a police officer, and an attorney from the Office of Commonwealth's attorney would talk to the students and their attending parent guardian about driving responsibly. These ceremonies were

[01:56:10] conducted for 90 minutes, three days a week for 50 weeks of the year, serving approximately 150 students at each ceremony. This was an impactful experience for students and parents to absorb the weight of the responsibility each driver takes on when they get behind the wheel. It also reminded juveniles that driving in Virginia is a privilege and that until they turn 18, it's a privilege granted and controlled by their parent or guardian. The judge then handed the license to the parent or guardian. In 2022, due to the pandemic, the General Assembly amended the code requirements allowing the chief judge to waive the ceremonial requirements or mandate that the ceremonies be

[01:56:48] conducted in an alternative way. Given the strain on court resources and the need to bring JDR court cases to finality within six months, the judge decided to suspend driver's license ceremonies, and they are now meeting their six-month target, which is a good thing. Over 50 jurisdictions in Virginia, including Arlington and Alexandria, have returned the driver's license ceremony.

[01:57:12] Since 2022, we've tragically witnessed several car accidents involving student drivers. In light of these accidents and the prevalence of dangerous driving in our region, I recently met with the JDR chief judge to discuss reinstating these driver's license ceremonies.

[01:57:29] Chief Judd agreed that we need to do something to address the student driver's safety, including addressing distracted driving. He supports the efforts to reinstate the driver's license ceremony in a fashion that accommodates more students at a time to cut down on needed court resources.

[01:57:44] Rather than smaller ceremonies at courthouses in discussion with the chief judge, we're considering fewer larger early evening ceremonies at school auditoriums throughout the county to reduce the administrative effort and make it easier for residents to attend.

[01:58:02] These ceremonies serve as a touchpoint with our young drivers and a chance to instill the importance of driving safety. Surrounding jurisdictions are recognizing the same thing and have restated their ceremonies as well. I am continuing to work with the courts, Chief Davis, several of you, as well as FCPS, and will be bringing it to the county executive and deputy county executive for public safety into the conversation to look at bringing these ceremonies back.

[01:58:31] And it may require a small consideration item to fund a support position for the courts. I welcome your feedback. [02:24:30] We'll begin with the background and board authorization for this plan amendment. The proposed facilities are included in the county's adopted capital improvement program, reflecting longstanding public facility needs. On December 5th, 2023, the Board of Supervisors authorized this plan amendment to evaluate the co-location of public facility uses on the subject property, which include a replacement facility for the existing Penn Doll fire station and emergency shelter with supportive housing for individuals and families.

[02:24:59] Together, these facilities will modernize critical public safety infrastructure and expand access to the emergency shelter and supportive housing in a central trans-accessible location along the Richmond Highway Corridor. The subject property is a 3.5-acre site located along the south side of Beacon Hill Road and is the former side of the Hydeville Valley Nursery.

[02:25:19] A portion of the site or the subject property is located within the Beacon Grove and CBC. Additionally, the subject property is within a walking distance of a planned VRT station along Richmond Highway. The site is surrounded by single-family residential to the east, north and south, and by retail uses to the west.

[02:25:37] The plan amendment supports an option for a co-located fire station and emergency and supportive housing facility. up to an intensity of 0.6 FAR. This would support a three-story, approximately 55,000-square-foot emergency and supportive housing facility with 50 shelter beds, 20 supportive housing units for individuals, and 10 supportive housing units for families, along with a two-story, approximately 24,000-square-foot fire station to replace the existing PennDOT fire station.

[02:26:07] The plan text also includes guidance on site design, including transition and buffering to adjacent residential neighborhoods, as well as multimodal improvements along Beacon Hill Road. This concludes the staff presentation for the plan amendment. I will now turn it over to Tabitha Cole, who will present the concurrent rezoning application.

[02:26:25] Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Good afternoon. I am Tabitha Cole with the Department of Planning and Development, as statedably presenting the concurrent zoning application. The site is zoned to the C8 district, which is shown in red on this slide, and R3, which would be shown in yellow.

[02:26:44] And the entirety of the site is located within the highway corridor overlay. The applicant proposes to rezone the site to the R8 District to permit the development of two public facility buildings with a 0.52 FAR. The western portion of the site would be developed with a three-story emergency and supportive housing building, and the eastern portion of the site would be developed with a two-story fire station.

[02:27:08] The fire station and housing facility would be accessed independently of each other with no shared vehicular or internal connection. The site's frontage would be improved with a five-foot-wide sidewalk, six-foot-wide bike lane, and a bus shelter. The site would have one and a quarter acre of open space, as shown on this slide.

[02:27:30] Recreational amenities for the residents of the supportive housing facility are provided at the rear of the facility. Stormwater would be stored and treated on-site through the use of an underground tension and filtering facility, which would then be released into an existing storm sewer south of the site.

[02:27:49] Required landscaping would be provided along the peripheries of the site, within the parking areas, and supplemented with shade structures topped with photovoltaic panels. [02:28:39] Good afternoon. My name is Claudia Shalhoub, and I am the project manager with the Department of Public Works and Environmental Services. [02:29:01] I would like to first extend my gratitude to Tabitha Cole, David Stinson, and the staff of the Department of Planning and Development for their guidance in processing this application. The project has undergone extensive community engagement since 2020.

[02:29:34] The project team has worked with the advisory committee and the broader community. Three concepts were presented, and the current design incorporates the community and advisory committee's feedback. We have also met with the property owners that are to the south of the project site to discuss impact, provide detail on landscape buffer and stormwater management, and address questions.

[02:29:55] From the onset, the project website was created to keep the community informed about the project milestones and schedule of upcoming community meetings. We also installed an information board on the site with a QR code that provides direct access to the project website.

[02:30:12] Site benefits include sufficient area to accommodate both uses. The site proximity to Route 1 and public transportation offer employment opportunities for shelter and supportive housing clients. A temporary fire station is not required since the proposed site is located one block from the existing fire station.

[02:30:30] This project will provide an opportunity to greatly improve the quality and quantity of existing stormwater conditions. The location of the program on site was developed with input from the advisory community using agencies and consistent with the adopted comprehensive plan by placing the taller three-story emergency shelter and supportive housing to the west closer to Richmond Highway, the two-story fire station on the eastern portion of the site, creating transition from higher density along Richmond Highway to lower density residential neighborhood.

[02:31:07] In coordination with the comments received from the advisory committee and community, the architectural design of the development respects the character of the surrounding residential neighborhood, yet clearly reflects a modern civic facility, creating an exciting architectural addition to the community.

[02:31:25] Amenities have been incorporated across the site to support each programmatic use, which have been reviewed and coordinated with the using agencies. The supportive housing amenities shown on the upper view include children's playground, turf area in the center for playing, and area for social gathering.

[02:31:42] The shelter amenities shown in the lower view include entry plazas, an outdoor space with picnic table, outdoor seating, and workout equipment. As staff mentioned, a retaining wall is required on the east and south portion of the site. To mitigate the visual impact of the high wall, the slide shows the southeast highest point of the retaining wall with the buffer depicted at one year growth in the lower image and 10 years fully grown in the upper image, indicating the amount of screening provided to reduce the visual impact of the wall.

[02:32:18] In addition, the project team has incorporated stepped retaining wall along portion of the east and southern side of the retaining wall with evergreen planting to provide maximum near-round screening. Both buildings are designed to be high-performing and environmentally friendly, targeting LEED Gold certification.

[02:32:37] An efficient mechanical system for HVAC, combined with enhanced envelope design, reduced the baseline energy use. Rooftop solar panel offset the remaining energy load to achieve net zero energy in both buildings. To reduce the reliance on fossil fuel, both facilities are designed to be all-electric buildings with electric vehicle charging stations provided on site.

[02:33:02] In summary, the Penda Fire Station Emergency Shelter and Supportive Housing Project is a critical investment to the future of the community. The extensive community engagement helped shape the design of the project by co-locating the fire station with the shelter and supportive housing.

[02:33:20] We are supporting county goals by enhancing public safety and supporting the population experiencing homelessness and promoting sustainable development. Thank you for your time and consideration. We are happy to answer any questions. Thank you very much. Are there any questions of staff or the applicant at this time?

[02:33:39] Supervisor Stewart. Well, first off, this, as you can tell, has been a long process and one that I think has really served all of us very, very well. It's never easy, particularly to not only move a fire station, but also to establish a new emergency housing and supportive housing.

[02:35:36] The proposed design will capture the majority of stormwater in a large underground storm tech facility, which is the one outlined in blue, and will release it at a lower and controlled rate. The stormwater will flow from proposed pipe from the new stormwater management facility to the existing county storm system.

[02:35:59] The reduced rate of flow will substantially reduce the impact to the existing system and will also reduce both the volume and velocity of stormwater runoff that is currently sheet flowing over vegetated area from the site to the neighboring homes.

[02:36:15] This new stormwater management also will include filtering system that will enhance the water quantity prior to discharge into public stormwater collection system. [03:19:35] For your consideration, our miscellaneous amendments to the public facilities manual. The amendments are broken into two parts, the sanitary sewer amendment and minor and editorial updates. The sanitary sewer amendment updates the design flows and peaking factors that developers use to design sanitary sewers and updates the associated plates and tables in the PFM.

[03:20:01] The amendment was a joint effort between DPWES and DPD and with industry outreach, resulting in a recommendation that the PFM's sanitary design flows are too high and should be adjusted downward to be consistent with industry practice and development trends.

[03:20:20] Minor and editorial updates are also proposed. These changes are necessary to bring the PFM in alignment with current ordinances, state standards, and current practices. They include edits to terminology and standards to align with the county's erosion and stormwater management ordinance and the Virginia Stormwater Handbook.

[03:20:43] Edits to bring the PFM in alignment with the zoning ordinance's new and updated definitions for townhouses. Plate 20-7, related to driveway entrances, is being updated to clarify VDOT standards for accepting new private driveways along their existing roadways.

[03:21:06] And lastly, the sump pump language being proposed for inclusion into the PFM brings the PFM current with LDS's longstanding practice where designers consider the location of the sump pump discharge point early in the design process so that it does not become an issue in the field.

[03:49:01] Good afternoon, Mr. Chairman, members of the board. My name is Bill Barrett, and I'm the Director of Wastewater and Planning and Monitoring Division. The proposed sewer charge increases for fiscal year 2027 are primarily driven by the reinvestment needs of aging infrastructure within the counties and our treatment-by-contract partner systems.

[03:49:20] The proposed increases are as follows. An availability charge increase of 2% or $180 for single-family dwelling units, $144 for multifamily dwelling units, and accordingly for other types of connections. A sewer service charge increase of 5.89%, or $0.55 per 1,000 gallons.

[05:24:05] energy prices don't just make people poor. They strip away the financial buffers. They keep a household from collapsing. Energy is a non-discretionary expense. You cannot opt out of heating your home in the winter or cooling it in the summer. You cannot opt out of the fuel required to drive to a job that puts food on the table. [05:36:33] The first part of this, I apologize, will be a touch pedantic. I'm giving you some charts. This is a background of the Fairfax County pension system. The pension system reports define normal cost as the percentage of payroll required to fund projected benefits, and employers and members contribute.

[05:36:48] These costs are invested to grow pension assets that will pay for future employee benefits. The present value, using our discount rate of 6.75% of projected employee benefits, is called the pension liability. As reported in the ACFERS, when the value of pension liability drops below 90% of the pension liability, the assets, 90% of the pension liability, the employer must recognize an unfunded liability as a debt that must be repaid.

[05:37:13] All Fairfax County pension systems have been below 90% for more than 20 consecutive years. Every year, the employer has been paying to support the normal cost of employee benefits and amortization payments, debt payments towards the unfunded liability.

[05:37:29] It is presumed that if the rate of return meets or exceeds the discount rate while making unfunded liability amortization payments, this liability will eventually be retired. However, unfunded liabilities emerge when not only the rate of investment return is less than discount rate, but when we have negative adjustments to actuarial projections and excessive asset return volatility and other changes impacting liability projections.

[05:37:54] On your next chart, you'll see that over the last, according to the Chiron report, over the last five and 15 years, we've met or exceeded our discount rate in our rate of return. And yet, all of our pension systems since 2010 are between 80% and 120% higher.

[05:38:12] And over the past five years, they've gone up 25% to 30%. So other variables have driven the unfunded liability higher despite acceptable asset rates of return. On the next chart, you're going to see the impact of all of this. So on these charts, I show a green line at the bottom of the charts.

[05:38:29] Those are the service costs, which include the normal cost of supporting our employee benefits. The red line is what the employer has been committing. So these pension systems are two and a half to three and a half times higher today than what they would be if we were in balance.

[05:38:47] If you add in the ERFC, if we can get these pension systems into balance, it would free up more than $400 million a year. So my conclusion and recommendation, I would say that there has been comments that the unfunded liability as long as we're 75% or 80% funded, I think we're okay.

[06:35:43] That's nearly 6 percentage points of additional benefit from the same pool of dollars. And the model accounts for different priorities. We tested four weighting scenarios. [06:36:03] And here are all the five scenarios sorted by total award value. Every optimized model beats uniform allocation, which is the gray bar on the left. And two models stood out to me.
